It is read from the held file when the option is turned on (behind the parents' code and the second factor) or with "Confirm", and compared on the same held file right before the start, after hashing and with the lock read again; a different digest, a rule without a confirmation or a pin in an older format does not start there (the Companion apps page says so and offers "Confirm"). "Confirm" first shows the checksum (12 hex digits), size, date, signature and version; the second factor is bound to that digest and is refused if the file changed since it was shown. On an open couch the pin does not apply (a program that updates itself keeps starting for adults); Wand keeps its signature rule. If the program file, its folder, the working folder or a folder above them can be changed by non-administrators (write in the folder, inherited write access for files only (OI) including inherit-only entries, and delete, rename, change permissions or take ownership further up the chain; ACLs that cannot be read count as changeable), the app does not start on the locked couch. This is checked again at every start, not only at confirmation; a folder that becomes writable later blocks the start. The page shows the state and "Confirm" offers "Allow anyway" (off by default; the choice is part of the digest and of the second factor, a hand-set field breaks the pin). Limit: only the program file, the folders (program folder, working folder and the chain above) and their inherited entries are judged. Per-file ACLs on DLLs or other files next to the program, and subfolders the program loads from (mods, plugins), are not judged. **Limit:** the pin protects against a swapped file or a changed start line, not against a process of the same user editing `config.json`; same-user file access is out of scope, as before.
- **Media players (Kodi, Emby, Jellyfin, Plex):** Only names and a full-screen parameter in the start rules and the running-detection, nothing shipped, nothing downloaded, nothing controlled. A profile grants no rights: the player is an app, and on the locked couch its start counts as an app. A process is recognized by name only, outside the Windows folder.

### Plugins (from 0.6)

Plugins run inside CouchHUD's process with the same rights. **There is no sandbox.** What CouchHUD does instead:
- **Who may do what (functions, from 0.6):** A plugin can declare named functions and tag pages, rows, menu entries, tabs and tools with them. Per function the owner picks *Admin only*, *Restricted too* (locked couch, child profile) or *Nobody*. The host checks the level before every show and every run; the plugin is never asked, and what the UI hides does not replace the check. Unknown functions and damaged or foreign values in the configuration count as *Admin only*, never looser. A plugin's suggestion counts above *Admin only* for CouchHUD's own plugins only; a third-party plugin cannot release anything for itself, only the owner can. *Nobody* hides and refuses calls, those of the assistant and MCP too. An admin function in a child profile needs the parents' code for a page, row or menu entry, once for exactly that plugin, function and entry (the couch code never stands in there; a page visit counts only for the same page, only in the same profile, at most five minutes, and ends when the page is left, on a profile switch, the lock and a game start; a plugin's settings page counts as one page with its own visit id $settings: a visit there covers all admin settings of that plugin, across functions, while it lasts, and plugin pages whose id starts with $ are not accepted; a row or menu entry without an admin page asks on every press; the host keeps the visit, the page can only end it; the actions `pluginPage`, `pluginRow` and `pluginAction` are not in `HudOnlyActions`); tools of the assistant and MCP cannot ask for a code, so admin is refused for them in a child profile. Changing a level is a change of protection: only on the HUD's own page, never on the locked couch or in a child profile, with the parents' code (the unlock when there is none). Without parental controls the existing rule for the HUD's own actions applies: the couch code stands in for the parents' code (`pluginFunction` is in `HudOnlyActions`), but only while the service, asked fresh, holds no parents' code; with parental controls it stays the parents' code. Without parental controls there are no child profiles, and the couch code is the highest protection there is. Plugin settings (`setPluginSetting`) are in `LockPolicy.OpenWhileLocked` so that a setting of level *Restricted too* works on the locked couch as well; the host (`Hud.SetPluginSetting`) still decides per setting: *Admin only* (a setting without a function too, even with a `$settings` visit) and *Nobody* are refused there, and the value must fit the kind of the setting. The entries go with the plugin. A settings entry that opens a plugin page (`SettingKind.Page`, `openPluginSettingPage`, SDK 4 and newer): the host looks the entry up fresh by plugin and key, checks the page against the pages of the same plugin (never a core page with `$`, never another plugin's), cleans the label as for page actions and decides with the same level as for a setting (on the locked couch only for *Restricted too*, in a child profile never a `$settings` visit); the entry only decides whether it may be opened (before the page is looked at, so locked and child callers get the same answer), then only the page asks for the parents' code once with its own function and begins a visit of that page id only, so the plugin's address and token stay locked. A plugin can register at most 64 settings; plugin text in log lines is cleaned and shortened. The UI never names a page, and the call is on none of the lists of the assistant, MCP and the phone remote.

- **Origin:** every catalog package carries a signed statement (ECDSA P-256, its own context `couchhud-plugin-v1`, not interchangeable with catalog or release signatures). Signed with CouchHUD's key it is an own plugin; signed with a developer's key it is a third-party plugin. That key is pinned per plugin id on first install (TOFU); an update with another key, an older version, a bad signature or a package that does not match its statement is refused and never re-pinned. The way out is to uninstall and deliberately reinstall, with the warning again.
- **Capabilities:** every member of the plugin context checks on every call whether its capability is granted (a test makes sure none is missing). At most what was shown and recorded at install time is granted. Handing over a setup, minimizing programs on the locked couch and adapting autostart arguments are for CouchHUD's own plugins only; run-time code never for third parties. Command lines only for a program below Program Files that the parents confirmed for exactly this plugin, with limited arguments without control characters.
- **The lint is not a sandbox:** before installing, CouchHUD reads the code's metadata (without running it) and refuses programs and scripts in the package and code that uses processes, the registry, the network or native libraries without declaring it. For third parties everything that gets around the restriction to the SDK and reaches CouchHUD's objects also counts as run-time code (and is refused): expression trees (`System.Linq.Expressions`, also `Compile` on a generic type), `dynamic` (`Microsoft.CSharp.RuntimeBinder`), `FieldInfo`/`PropertyInfo` `GetValue`/`SetValue`, `Assembly.GetTypes`/`DefinedTypes`/`ExportedTypes`, `Delegate.CreateDelegate`, `AppDomain.GetAssemblies`, `Assembly.GetCallingAssembly`/`GetEntryAssembly`/`GetExecutingAssembly`, `Type.GetField`/`GetProperty`/`GetMethod`/`GetMember`, `UnsafeAccessor` and Visual Basic's late binding (`Microsoft.VisualBasic` and `.Core` are not base library for them, because `Interaction.Shell` starts programs without naming `Process`). Every pattern has a test. Code that hides what it calls gets past all of that anyway: reflection in the same process stays possible. Hence the pinned key, the warning and revocation.
- **No host assemblies:** no plugin may reference CouchHUD's own assemblies (Core, Shell, service); the lint refuses it and the loader loads them for no plugin. Third-party packages may reference only the SDK, the .NET base library and their own DLLs (their own by the name they define, not by file name). From the default context the loader hands out only the SDK (name compared ignoring case) and, for plugins that are not CouchHUD's own, only the .NET runtime libraries, never CouchHUD's own libraries (WebView2, QRCoder, the service controller); a plugin brings whatever else it needs. This is no sandbox either: code in the same process can still reach CouchHUD's objects through reflection.
- **Gates:** installing, updating, enabling, disabling, approving, uninstalling and confirming a program only from CouchHUD's own UI, never from the remote, MCP, the assistant or plugins, never on the locked couch and never in a child profile. Skins are covered by the same gate (no install or update in a child profile). The page asks the host first (`getPluginGate`) and shows a refusal (locked couch, child profile, expert mode, developer switch) before any code or administrator prompt. Third-party plugins only in expert mode and always with the parents' code (the couch code without parental controls); unsigned packages and approving a folder put there by hand additionally only with the developer switch. With a broken `config.json` none of this changes anything; the parents' code is used up only after success. An update that wants new capabilities counts as a new install. Approving a folder put there by hand holds for exactly the files whose code the dialog showed (the shown hash is compared with the fresh one in constant time; if anything was swapped it is refused) and checks the code like an install. Catalog packages are handled in one go: hash, code check and unpacking run over the same open handle, which keeps writers out, and the hash of the unpacked files (before they move into place) is what is remembered.
- **Plugins without a restart (from 0.7):** installing, approving, switching on, switching off and uninstalling take effect at once; an update stays with the restart (the running plugin's DLLs are in use, the old code cannot be shown to be gone in bounded time, and an update with new capabilities is a new approval). **One code path:** starting at once calls `PluginManager.LoadFolder`, the very method the start (`LoadAll`) uses: approved list, removal mark, revocation list, switch, Offline mode, SDK, id rules, capabilities (`PluginPolicy.Granted`) and function levels; the gates (unlocked, no child profile, expert mode, parents' code, broken `config.json`) come before as ever and are used up only after success. **Threat model.** (1) A swap between check and load: `PluginFolderSnapshot` opens every file of the folder once with `FileShare.Read` (nobody can write, replace, delete or rename the folder), computes the hash from those handles (the same as `PluginTrust.HashFolder`), compares it with the signed list and keeps the handles as long as the plugin runs; the load context loads only paths that were hashed (a DLL put there afterwards is refused). Links inside the folder and files that cannot be held keep the folder from loading (fail closed). The folders of the plugin are held too (they cannot be renamed or replaced by a link), and every handle is compared with the expected path through `GetFinalPathNameByHandle`. **Native libraries:** every plugin assembly gets its own `DllImport` resolver (`NativeLibrary.SetDllImportResolver`, at the normal start too): a library from the plugin folder is loaded only if it was hashed; a file of the same name that shows up in the folder later is refused with `DllNotFoundException`, and for other developers' plugins anything else may only be a system library by plain name from System32 (never the search of the assembly folder, PATH or the working folder). CouchHUD's own plugins are the exception: they may also use the normal search after the folder was checked. Before every load the folder tree is searched: if a file lies there that was not checked, nothing is loaded (fail closed, with a log line), and the library is loaded with the search flags "its own folder plus System32". **Honestly:** Windows resolves the dependencies of a native DLL itself; the check narrows the window but does not close it. `NativeLibrary.Load` or `LoadLibrary` from the plugin's own code is not covered at all: the barrier protects against third parties swapping files, not against the plugin itself. Limit: a native library, once loaded, stays in the process until CouchHUD quits, also after the plugin was stopped or uninstalled (Windows does not release it while any code uses it); a plugin that loaded one therefore does not start again in the same run (nor does a new package in the same folder), only after a CouchHUD restart. (2) A half-loaded plugin with rights: while `Initialize` runs, its registrations are invisible to lists, tabs, tools and calls; the entry and the registrations appear in one step (under one lock), and when the start fails or hangs (30 seconds) everything is taken back (rights, pages, tools, functions, providers, tabs, settings, restricted programs, startup adapters); calls the plugin makes afterwards go nowhere. (3) A plugin that hangs or misbehaves while stopping: it is cut off first (the instance's context is dead, every call into the host is ignored quietly, never an exception, so a thread of the plugin cannot crash CouchHUD; events no longer reach it), then `Dispose` is called with a 5 second limit and not waited for any longer; the lock for load and stop operations is free afterwards. A plugin that does not confirm counts as not stopped: its folder does not start again in the same run (two copies never side by side), its files stay held. **Limit:** what a plugin started itself (threads, timers, sockets, processes) CouchHUD cannot end inside the same process; until the restart the UI says so explicitly. After the stop its settings read as empty and are not written any more (the store is not created again for that instance) and no data folder is made for it; calls and event subscriptions are judged under the same lock the host takes to cut the instance off, so a registration cannot slip in between; all its subscriptions to the host's events are taken back. The same plugin id in another folder does not start while an earlier run is not proven stopped. If a start fails and the plugin cannot be stopped with a confirmation, its files stay held and folder and id are blocked until the restart. Texts from a plugin (error texts, reasons, log lines) are cut to one line without control characters and 200 characters before they are shown or logged. Right before the immediate start after an install the host asks its gates (lock, child profile, expert mode) once more; if one has closed in the meantime the plugin starts with the next start. (4) Rights: capabilities and function levels are worked out at the start by hand as at the start and checked on every call; a plugin gains nothing from starting at once that a restart would not give it. The load context is collectible (`isCollectible`) so memory and files are released after a clean stop; that is a request, not a promise. Tests: `PluginHotLoadTests`.
- **The parents' grant is used up before the plugin's code runs (on purpose):** for rows, steppers, choices and quick actions that ask for the parents' code, the grant is used up before the plugin's code runs, not after. The host cannot see whether a plugin did what the parents allowed; a grant that stayed after the call could pay for a second press. A press the host refuses itself beforehand (locked, broken `config.json`, invalid value) uses up nothing. A press that then fails inside the plugin costs the code. This is not a bug and is not to be "fixed": the contracts in the tests (`ConfigFaultDriftContractTests`) pin the order.
- **Locked couch:** plugin pages show only what the plugin marked for it; only rows of CouchHUD's own plugins can be used there. Quick actions and assistant tools run there only if one of CouchHUD's own plugins marked them for it (`AllowedWhileLocked`); the flag of any other plugin is ignored, and the guide menu shows only the marked entries while locked. The parents' code still applies where an entry asks for it. Link rows are opened by CouchHUD itself, only to an existing page of the same plugin, without calling the plugin's code. What a page returns is bounded (number of rows, text lengths, images only as PNG up to 256 KB and 4096 pixels a side); a changed value must be one of the offered options or a step inside the stepper's range.
- **Plugin tabs (capability `tabs`):** a plugin describes data only (id, title, icon, area, a page of the same plugin); CouchHUD draws everything, never HTML or script of the plugin. Attackers: a plugin that passes for a built-in tab ("Settings" or "Parents", say), floods the tab bar or pushes out a built-in one, a child who reaches a page that is not theirs through a plugin, and a plugin with an image or text that attacks the interface. The host (`PluginTabRules`) allows at most two tabs per plugin and six in all (CouchHUD's own plugins first, so others cannot push them out), the tabs always come after the built-in ones and before Settings, never replace or hide a built-in tab, and reserved titles (built-in tabs, the names of CouchHUD's plugins, look-alikes with digits or separators, part of a longer title, non-Latin scripts) are refused; titles are limited to 14 characters and to Latin letters (umlauts and accents are folded to their base letter and compared that way; look-alikes such as ı, ł or ǀ and other scripts are refused), digits and a few signs, images are accepted only as a checked PNG (64 KB, 256 pixels), and everything in the interface is set as text. A glyph is exactly one emoji, arrow or shape (no letters, digits, joiners or direction characters); other plugins cannot take one that CouchHUD draws as its own icon (lock, gear ...), they get a dot. The area "always" is for CouchHUD's own plugins only, for any other it counts as apps, so a switched off area always hides their tab. The page checks the list again. A tab shows only while the plugin is loaded and holds the capability; without it the registration already throws. A switched off area hides its plugin tabs. A child's profile has only the tabs of CouchHUD's own plugins that mark themselves `ChildSafe` (the flag does nothing for any other plugin; the old Smart Home tab was visible to children, and the plugin's tab is meant to stay so); the locked couch keeps only the tabs of CouchHUD's own plugins. Switching off and uninstalling remove the tab at once (the host asks the state and the removed mark anew on every query). The page behind the tab uses the same calls and the same lock and parents' code rules as any plugin page; on the locked couch it shows only what is released and allows only rows of CouchHUD's own plugins. The call `getPluginTabs` only reads; the phone remote, MCP and the assistant cannot reach it (tests `PluginTabsTests`).
- **Revocation:** the signed catalog can revoke versions or a developer key; such plugins do not load until the parents allow them again for exactly those files.
- **Serial (replay protection):** the signed index carries a whole number `serial` that must rise with every change (`scripts/catalog-sign.ps1` raises it by 1 before signing; the signature covers it). A fetched index with a **lower** serial than the saved copy of the same address is refused: the copy (and with it the revocation list) stays, and the event shows in the error line and the log. An equal serial is fine, a missing field counts as 0. Every catalog address has its own counter; the check runs only when both sides (the new index and the saved copy) pass their signature, that is for the default catalog and catalogs with a pinned key. It does not run for an unsigned foreign catalog, since any number there can be made up and a high one would lock out its real updates. A changed copy in the cache (invalid signature) does not count. Limit: whoever can delete the saved copy (the same user) resets the counter, and the very first fetch has nothing to compare with; an index is also never "expired", only never older than the last one seen. Older CouchHUD versions (0.5) keep reading the index and ignore the field.
- **Approval list:** `plugin-trust.json` (format 2) is protected by an HMAC under this Windows user's key. An old list (format 1) is taken over without granting new rights. A list that names a folder twice in different case (`Foo` and `foo`) is damaged (nothing unknown loads, the file stays unchanged), never an exception. Plugins that were in the user folder before the list existed are taken over exactly once: at the first start after the update from 0.5.x or older (marker `PluginTrustAdopted` in `config.json`; old files lack it, and it is set as soon as the list exists). After that a missing list adopts nothing; a folder dropped in stays "unknown". If the key cannot be read, an existing list counts as damaged: nothing unknown loads, nothing is written. A staged update goes in at the next start only if its SHA-256 matches the entry under the HMAC (not the one in `catalog-installed.json`). An uninstalled plugin whose files are still loaded gets a removal mark under the HMAC; it no longer loads and is deleted at the next start before any plugin loads. It does not protect against a program running as the same user.
- **Media:** icons and screenshots come only from a local cache after SHA-256 (from the signed statement), PNG format, file size and image dimensions (at most 4096 pixels a side and 16 megapixels) are checked; never straight from the network.
- **Catalogs:** CouchHUD's own catalog (`raw.githubusercontent.com/kewe87/CouchHUD-Catalog`, signed with CouchHUD's key) is always there and can neither be removed nor switched off, not even by an entry in `config.json` (it is always read first). Further catalogs (`ExtraCatalogSources`, at most 8) get in only through the HUD's own UI: never on the locked couch or in a child's profile, only in expert mode, with the parents' code (the couch code without parental controls), only over https without a user name, and only after the explicit confirmation "I trust this developer" (the bridge passes on only a real `true`), because whoever runs the catalog or maintains a plugin in it could slip in malicious code. Optionally the catalog's key is pinned (then `index.json.sig` is verified with it, otherwise the index counts as unsigned). Everything from it is third-party: a package carrying CouchHUD's signature is refused from there, a plugin without its developer's signature is not installed, the names of CouchHUD's plugins (and look-alikes) and every id of the own catalog are reserved, and a foreign catalog can never revoke anything. An index may be 512 KB at most and is cut off after 200 entries. An entry's category (`category`) is display only (a skin can only carry `skins` or `hud`): it is part of the signed index, decides nothing (no rights, no check), only known ids are accepted and the names come from the program, never as text from the index. A hand-edited `config.json` does not get past the rules: invalid entries (http, double, too many, invalid key) are not loaded. The old `CatalogSources` list only has an effect with the developer switch. A pinned key is a promise: if the signature is missing or does not fit (also for the cached copy), the catalog is not used. Addresses that lead into your own network (localhost, private, link-local and CGNAT addresses, names without a dot, `.local` and the like) are not allowed as a catalog, download or image (except with the developer switch); the client follows no redirects by itself, the host follows at most three by hand and checks every hop again, and when connecting the resolved addresses are checked once more (an IPv4 address inside an IPv6 one such as NAT64, 6to4, Teredo and `::a.b.c.d` counts as the inner IPv4 address). Behind a proxy (the Windows setting is used) the connection goes to the proxy, which may sit in the home network, and of the target addresses only the name is checked (no protection against DNS rebinding; the proxy resolves it). So catalogs of other developers (index, downloads, images) are used behind a proxy only with the developer switch and are otherwise skipped with a message; CouchHUD's own hosts work through the proxy. Redirects lead only to https (with the developer switch also to http on this PC), never to `file:` or other schemes. Icons and screenshots from a foreign catalog are fetched only when the package statement checks out (never from the unsigned index), within 15 seconds overall and at most 100 entries. Names may not contain control, bidi or zero-width characters and may not pose as CouchHUD or "official". A skin from a foreign catalog goes through the same gate as a third-party plugin. After adding or removing, the loaded list is dropped; nothing is installed from a removed catalog. Until CouchHUD's own catalog has loaded once, plugins from foreign catalogs cannot be installed (on top of a fixed list of reserved names in the program).
- **Free plugins only:** a free SPDX licence is required, price and purchase fields are refused.
- **Home Assistant as a plugin:** the plugin's credentials are kept encrypted (Windows account, additionally bound to the plugin id) in its settings; a value that looks like an encrypted one is never stored as plain text, and without encryption a secret is not stored at all. The credentials from 0.5 (address, choice, pinned entries, encrypted token or sign-in) are handed over by the program once, at the plugin's first start: the token is encrypted again, never passed on in plain text, and only for CouchHUD's own plugin (reserved id); after a successfully written hand-over the old encrypted token fields in the configuration are cleared (no second copy; on a failure they stay for another try), the other old fields are never written or used again. A plugin's web sign-in (`browser.signin`, own plugins only) is described in the section "Web sign-in of a plugin". The plugin binds every credential to the address it was made for.
- **Home Assistant: the Home Assistant user's rights decide.** Whoever has a token for Home Assistant can do exactly what that user may do in Home Assistant; a second lock in CouchHUD would be for show. So all actions (tiles, guide menu entries, the assistant tool, every kind of device, gates, scripts, automations and locks too) are allowed on the locked couch as well. Everything that changes the setup stays behind the unlock: address, token, signing in and out, the choice of devices and pinning. **Parents who want limits set them in Home Assistant** (a user of their own with the rights the couch should have, and that user's token or sign-in). There is no Home Assistant integration in the program itself any more (deleted in 0.6, the assistant tool and the bridge calls too); without the plugin CouchHUD knows nothing of Home Assistant.
- **CaseHUD as a plugin:** the plugin talks only to CaseHUD on this PC (`127.0.0.1`, no proxy, no redirects, token and port only from `api.json`, which counts as untrusted: size, spelling, port, token, the process id belongs to the installed `CaseHUD.exe`, same session, started before the file was written). The token is in no log, no text and no row. The plugin starts programs only through the verified `CaseHUD.exe` (HKLM entry, real path through the file handle, no link, below Program Files; never a path from `api.json` or `instance.pid`), no shell, one argument (`--set-api=on|off`), 20 seconds, the process tree is ended, of its stderr at most one cleaned line of 200 characters goes to the log. Everything that changes something in CaseHUD or on the PC needs the unlock (the host checks it on the row, the plugin again); on the locked couch only looking, scene, next scene, power and finding the displays work, as before the move (the "Lighting" page is closed there now, even for looking). CaseHUD's windows are minimized on the locked couch as long as the plugin is loaded (`lock.restrict`, a restriction only, before the "unreadable, so allow" rule); the program's own CaseHUD check is gone, without the plugin the program does not know CaseHUD and treats its window like any unknown program. For CaseHUD the plugin may have `process`, `registry`, `native`, `setup.run`, `lock.restrict` and `startup.args`; the three services of the host exist for CouchHUD's own plugins only.
- **Gone: updating CaseHUD without UAC ("no-UAC", on purpose).** Through the CouchHUD service a signed CaseHUD setup could be installed as SYSTEM without a Windows prompt (`Operation.InstallCaseHud`). A plugin never gets a privileged power and this route cannot be moved into a plugin safely, so it is left out of the plugin. Updating is done in CaseHUD itself. The service route is deleted altogether (since 0.6): the operation, its check (only over an installed CaseHUD), the handler, the rate-limit entry, the call in the client, the restart flag of the job, and the tests. The service no longer knows the name `InstallCaseHud` and refuses it as an unknown operation; its attack surface is one operation smaller, and there is no second signature and key check for foreign setups any more (the service installs CouchHUD updates only).
- **Handing over a setup (`setup.run`, CouchHUD's own plugins only):** the plugin downloads the first installation of CaseHUD itself (only `github.com/kewe87/CaseHUD/releases/download/`, redirects only to GitHub hosts, at most 5; signed checksums with CaseHUD's own key and the context `casehud-release-v1`, DER format; size up to 400 MB, time limits; the hash is computed while loading). It gives file and hash to the host, which checks itself: unlocked, no child's profile; proof that someone pressed something (when a row or guide menu entry of that own plugin that asks for the parents' code is pressed, the host sets a one-shot token bound to the plugin id and valid for 35 minutes at most; without it there is no setup); one setup at a time; only an .exe in the own data folder of exactly this plugin (`plugin-data<id>`, never another plugin's), alone in its folder, by its normal path; the hash again on the held file; the real path; no link. It is started through the Windows admin prompt (`runas`, the system folder as working directory); a refusal there is a "no". Whoever swaps the file between plugin and host fails at the hash or at the held handle.
- **The VPN as a plugin:** the plugin (`vpn`, CouchHUD's own only) has the rights `pages`, `quickActions`, `process`, `registry` and `native`, no network, no assistant, no tab. It holds no credentials and asks no service for the address; it reads the state off the network adapter and the routing table. Threat model: the VPN app's program comes only from the HKLM entry, with its real path, no link, below Program Files; arguments separately (no shell), countries and servers only from the fixed list or the strict pattern; "Specific server" is a setting of the plugin and is checked again at every use. Locked couch: looking is marked, no change is (unlock, with parental controls the parents' code, and the page checks the lock again); the Guide menu entry is not shown while locked. The VPN in the program itself is deleted (since 0.6). The parents' code still applies to every change, in the plugin under the action "pluginRow"/"pluginAction" instead of "vpn".
- **Weather, news and the assistant's providers as plugins (0.7, SDK 5):** attacker: a plugin (or a feed, a weather or AI service behind one) that wants to reach other hosts, the home network or a stored key, or to put markup or instructions into the HUD. The core keeps the display and asks the plugins only for data through typed slots (`weather`, `news`, `assistant.provider`); every answer is bounded and cleaned (lengths, counts, control characters, http(s) links, https images only), every call has a timeout, a plugin's error text is never shown as it is. `assistant.provider` is CouchHUD's own plugins only (the provider sees the whole conversation including tool results); tool results stay marked as third-party data. Network: a plugin declares its services and hosts in `plugin.json`; the client from `IPluginContext.Http` lets a request through only to a declared host over https on its default port (only the `"*"` service may use http, on port 80/443), an answer is capped at 8 MB, only to public addresses (`SafeNet`, also after DNS), only while Offline is off, the service's own switch under Settings › Privacy is on (each declared service of a loaded plugin gets one, behind the parent code under parental controls) and the plugin is not paused; redirects are followed by the host (GET/HEAD, at most five, never https→http, each hop checked again) and headers that authorize the caller (`Authorization`, `x-api-key`, cookies) never go to another host. Fail closed: anything else is a `NetBlockedException`. Keys: the API keys move once into the provider plugin's secret store (decrypted and encrypted again for that plugin id, never plain), and only after the move worked are they removed from `config.json`; the settings page hands a new key over and never reads it back. Uninstalling a plugin deletes its stored secrets (an unreadable settings file goes as a whole); for Home Assistant that means signing in again after a reinstall. The services and hosts are part of the signed statement and the approval list; an update that widens them counts like a new permission, and the `plugin.json` may not declare more than the statement. A third-party SDK 5 plugin that declares services may not build a connection of its own (the lint refuses `HttpClient`, handlers, sockets, WebSocket, WebClient, Dns, Ping and SmtpClient at install); without services the Privacy switches do not apply to the plugin, which a note there says. Addresses that carry an IPv4 address (NAT64, 6to4, Teredo, compatible) are judged by the IPv4 inside. Limits: the lint is not a sandbox; code that hides what it calls gets past it (see below). CouchHUD's own plugins use only `Http`. Feeds on the home network no longer load (public addresses only).
- **Service allowed?, CouchHUD's state, stopping (0.7, SDK 5 additive):** attacker: a plugin that learns or may do more than it should through `ServiceAllowed`, that spies on the user's state, or that holds up the shutdown while CouchHUD quits. `ServiceAllowed(service)` is true only with Offline off, the service's switch on and the plugin not paused; fail closed: unknown, not declared, not approved, without `network:internet` or `network:lan`, `null`, and any exception while asking are `false`. The special token `"hosts": ["lan"]` (a target in the home network that the user types in) is in the signed statement and the approval list and counts as a new permission (parents' code, in the confirmation text, automatic updates ask); `"*"` does not include it and the other way round, and a third-party plugin gets `lan` only if it was approved. It is a declaration plus a Privacy switch, **not a technical fence** (the confirmation text says so too), and grants no reach: `Http` stays closed for the home network (a service with only `lan` gets no client, a host called `lan` never counts as a declared host), and the own sockets of a first-party plugin with `network:lan` cannot be fenced in by CouchHUD: there the switch is a promise of the plugin, which can only be checked for plugins that come from CouchHUD (third-party plugins with services may not build connections of their own). `host.state` shows, read only, the version, shell or app mode, PC mode, the lock, the end of the sleep timer and the running app (id and cleaned name): none of it is secret from a plugin that runs in this process, and the app's name is given only if the entry is not hidden and its area is on (the game event follows the same rule since 0.7: no event for hidden entries or switched-off areas, id and name cleaned; `InstallDir` stays a path for compatibility; child profiles are not filtered separately); there are no handles, process ids or actions, `Changed` is throttled (at most every 250 ms) and does not reach a paused plugin. It is a new capability with confirmation. `HostStopping` is not raised on a crash, a kill or a power loss and is not guaranteed (a plugin restores state at the next start too) and has a hard time limit (2 s for all plugins together, the handlers run side by side on the pool); a handler that hangs or throws is passed over and cannot hold up quitting (a hanging handler goes on in the background until the process ends), and the call is made once. `SettingChanged` names only the key, never the value. Brands (`samsungtv`, `anthropic`, `openai` and the earlier ones) are reserved for third parties (also with look-alike spellings; from five characters on also as part of a name and in the display name); plain words (`streaming`, `fancontrol`, `weather`, `news`, `wand`, `vpn`) only as the whole name or the first word (`weather-plus` is taken, `my-weather-widget` is free). A plugin entry whose id starts like the core's (`plugin:`, `steam:`, `dir:`) is skipped.
- **Plugins share the process and the Windows account (stated plainly):** the encryption of plugin secrets (DPAPI with the plugin id as extra entropy) keeps plugins apart in the files, but it is **no barrier against a malicious plugin**: it runs as the same Windows user as CouchHUD and could call DPAPI itself and read another plugin's file. So the lint refuses `ProtectedData` for third-party developers (CouchHUD's own plugins may use it), and the host encrypts for everybody else through `SetSecret`. That does not rule out detours (the lint is no sandbox). **Open point:** file access outside the plugin's own folder cannot be found reliably by a lint (paths are built at run time); only a separate process with reduced rights helps, which third-party plugins will need later.

### Signatures

Self-update and the plugin catalog are signed with ECDSA P-256. Fingerprint (SHA-256 over the public key, SPKI DER) of the self-update and catalog key:

`247bba62849ddfeab8749a38488791807bffa33c72347ea20931870dbbb7709f`

CaseHUD releases (`kewe87/CaseHUD`) are signed too (the CaseHUD plugin checks them, the program itself no longer does); key fingerprint:

`cfb6863f23c892a53c93786dd1ee78478618aed680c5603fbc3955c1dde4abaf`

See [docs/PLUGINS.md](docs/PLUGINS.md) and [docs/CASEHUD.md](docs/CASEHUD.md) (German).
